### Rethinking Graph Structure Learning in the Era of LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21223v1)
- **Authors**: Zhihan Zhang, Xunkai Li, Guang Zeng, Hongchao Qin, Ronghua Li, Guoren Wang
- **Abstract**: Recently, the emergence of large language models (LLMs) has prompted researchers to explore the integration of language descriptions into graphs, aiming to enhance model encoding capabilities from a data-centric perspective. This graph representation is called text-attributed graphs (TAGs). A review of prior advancements highlights that graph structure learning (GSL) is a pivotal technique for improving data utility, making it highly relevant to efficient TAG learning. However, most GSL methods are tailored for traditional graphs without textual information, underscoring the necessity of developing a new GSL paradigm. Despite clear motivations, it remains challenging: (1) How can we define a reasonable optimization objective for GSL in the era of LLMs, considering the massive parameters in LLM? (2) How can we design an efficient model architecture that enables seamless integration of LLM for this optimization objective? For Question 1, we reformulate existing GSL optimization objectives as a tree optimization framework, shifting the focus from obtaining a well-trained edge predictor to a language-aware tree sampler. For Question 2, we propose decoupled and training-free model design principles for LLM integration, shifting the focus from computation-intensive fine-tuning to more efficient inference. Based on this, we propose Large Language and Tree Assistant (LLaTA), which leverages tree-based LLM in-context learning to enhance the understanding of topology and text, enabling reliable inference and generating improved graph structure. Extensive experiments on 10 TAG datasets demonstrate that LLaTA enjoys flexibility - incorporated with any backbone; scalability - outperforms other LLM-based GSL methods in terms of running efficiency; effectiveness - achieves SOTA performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Rethinking Graph Structure Learning in the Era of LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of Graph Structure Learning (GSL) for text-attributed graphs (TAGs) in the context of large language models (LLMs). Recognizing the limitations of traditional GSL methods when dealing with the rich textual information in TAGs and the challenges of directly fine-tuning large LLMs, the authors propose a novel framework called Large Language and Tree Assistant (LLaTA).  LLaTA reformulates GSL as a tree-based optimization task, leveraging LLMs for in-context learning with topology-aware tree prompts. It consists of three main steps: topology-aware in-context construction (building a structural encoding tree), tree-prompted LLM inference (capturing semantic relationships), and leaf-oriented two-step sampling (refining the graph structure). The approach is designed to be decoupled and training-free, emphasizing reliable LLM inference over costly fine-tuning. Extensive experiments on 10 TAG datasets demonstrate that LLaTA achieves state-of-the-art performance, outperforming existing LLM-based GSL methods with better efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper presents a novel perspective on GSL in the LLM era.  The key idea of reformulating GSL as a tree-based optimization task and leveraging LLMs for *in-context* learning, guided by topology-aware prompts, is a significant departure from traditional edge prediction approaches.  The decoupled and training-free model design is also innovative, addressing the practical challenges of integrating large LLMs into GSL. The specific LLaTA implementation with its three-stage process appears original. The introduction of an encoding tree informed by structural entropy is clever and contributes to a more nuanced understanding of graph topology. The idea to use LLMs for semantic understanding within communities is also well-motivated and adds value.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a relevant and important problem. The integration of LLMs with graph learning is an active research area, and efficient and effective GSL techniques are crucial for harnessing the potential of TAGs. LLaTA offers a practical solution that could significantly impact how graph data is processed and understood, especially in domains where textual information is prevalent. The experimental results are compelling, showcasing LLaTA's superior performance and efficiency compared to existing methods. LLaTA's decoupled design offers significant advantages in terms of adaptability and scalability. This potentially lowers the barrier of entry for researchers and practitioners to experiment with GSL and LLMs, potentially spurring more adoption in applications that leverage TAGS.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-Motivated:**  The paper clearly articulates the challenges and opportunities of GSL in the LLM era, motivating the need for a new paradigm. The observation that current LLM-GSL methods tend to carry unnecessary complexities of traditional GSL is well founded.
    *   **Technically Sound:**  The proposed framework is well-defined and logically structured. The explanation of each component of LLaTA is clear and concise.
    *   **Extensive Experiments:**  The paper provides strong empirical evidence to support its claims.  The experiments are comprehensive, covering a diverse range of TAG datasets and comparing LLaTA against multiple baselines. Ablation studies provide insights into the importance of each component of LLaTA. Real-world scenario experiments also demonstrate the robustness of LLaTA.
    *   **Clear Presentation:**  The paper is well-written and easy to follow, with clear explanations of the technical concepts and experimental results. Figures and tables are used effectively to illustrate the key ideas and findings.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** While a general study is provided, it would improve the study to dive more into each dataset to understand how to optimally determine the correct K, , theta and r. While guidelines are stated, the specific optimal hyperparameters were not investigated in the experiment section.
    *   **Scalability to Extremely Large Graphs:** While LLaTA offers efficiency compared to other LLM-based methods, the paper does not provide an analysis of its scalability to extremely large graphs with millions or billions of nodes and edges. While many smaller graphs are experimented on, it would be useful to see a theoretical analysis of the limits to the proposed method, as well as potential optimizations to address these issues.
    *   **Dependence on structural encoding trees**: the reliance on constructing the structural entropy encoding tree may be a bottleneck if the underlying graph structure changes rapidly and recomputation is frequently required.

* **Potential Influence:**

   LLaTA has a high potential to influence the field of graph learning, particularly in areas where textual information plays a key role. The work's training-free and modular design makes it an attractive alternative to existing methods, and its strong experimental results suggest that it could become a widely adopted technique.

* The authors should include a discussion regarding potential ethical implications associated with their study.

**Score:** 8

**Justification:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field by proposing a novel, efficient, and effective framework for GSL in the LLM era.  The idea of leveraging LLMs for in-context learning with topology-aware prompts is both innovative and practical. The extensive experimental results provide strong evidence to support the claims of the paper. While there are minor weaknesses in terms of dataset sensitivity and a lack of detailed hyperparameter analysis in the experimental settings, the overall quality and potential impact of the work are high. The paper offers a valuable solution to a relevant problem and has the potential to influence future research in graph learning and LLM integration. The main limitations hold the work back from a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### LLaVA-CMoE: Towards Continual Mixture of Experts for Large Vision-Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21227v1)
- **Authors**: Hengyuan Zhao, Ziqin Wang, Qixin Sun, Kaiyou Song, Yilin Li, Xiaolin Hu, Qingpei Guo, Si Liu
- **Abstract**: Although applying Mixture of Experts to large language models for learning new tasks is widely regarded as an effective strategy for continuous learning, there still remain two major challenges: (1) As the number of tasks grows, simple parameter expansion strategies can lead to excessively large models. (2) Modifying the parameters of the existing router results in the erosion of previously acquired knowledge. In this paper, we present an innovative framework named LLaVA-CMoE, which is a continuous Mixture of Experts (MoE) architecture without any replay data. Specifically, we have developed a method called Probe-Guided Knowledge Extension (PGKE), which employs probe experts to assess whether additional knowledge is required for a specific layer. This approach enables the model to adaptively expand its network parameters based on task distribution, thereby significantly improving the efficiency of parameter expansion. Additionally, we introduce a hierarchical routing algorithm called Probabilistic Task Locator (PTL), where high-level routing captures inter-task information and low-level routing focuses on intra-task details, ensuring that new task experts do not interfere with existing ones. Our experiments shows that our efficient architecture has substantially improved model performance on the Coin benchmark while maintaining a reasonable parameter count.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLaVA-CMoE, a continual learning framework designed for large vision-language models. It addresses two key challenges in continual learning with Mixture of Experts (MoE): controlling model size and mitigating catastrophic forgetting in the router. LLaVA-CMoE employs Probe-Guided Knowledge Extension (PGKE) to dynamically allocate experts based on task requirements, and Probabilistic Task Locator (PTL), a hierarchical routing mechanism, to prevent new experts from interfering with existing ones. The paper demonstrates improved performance on the CoIN benchmark while maintaining a reasonable parameter count.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core contribution is the LLaVA-CMoE framework, which combines PGKE and PTL. While MoE architectures have been explored in continual learning previously (e.g., LEMOE, Lifelong-MoE), the proposed PGKE mechanism for adaptive expert allocation based on task probe guidance appears novel. The hierarchical routing mechanism (PTL) also presents a contribution by utilizing a VAE-based approach to task affiliation for the routers. PGKE stands out, as it decides on the number of experts based on the knowledge present in the existing experts, or if adding new experts is beneficial.
*   **Significance:** Continual learning in large vision-language models is a crucial area of research, and the paper tackles two practical challenges: model size and catastrophic forgetting. The demonstrated improvements on the CoIN benchmark suggest that the proposed methods are effective in these problems. The adaptive parameter allocation can lead to more efficient training and deployment of continual learning models.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly identifies two limitations of current continual learning approaches using MoEs.
    *   The proposed PGKE and PTL are well-motivated.
    *   Experimental results on CoIN demonstrate improved performance, which are well-documented with the tables.
    *   The paper also includes ablation studies to analyse the contributions of the PGKE and PTL in the methodology.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Some aspects of the method, such as the exact network architectures and hyperparameters, are only referred to in the Appendix.
    *   The reliance on a specific benchmark (CoIN) limits the generalizability of the results. While CoIN is specifically for CL, it is a newer benchmark. Experiments on more standard continual learning datasets could further validate the claims.
    *   The paper states, "Existing distillation or merging techniques often impact the router, leading to knowledge forgetting". However, there aren't any specific examples on the distillation that hinders the model's performance.
    *   The results on tasks such as TextVQA, do not exhibit high improvements compared to other tasks.
    *   The claim about outperforming "every-layer approach on OCRVQA" is only a 1.5% increase, which could be a result of the seed.

**Score:** 6/10

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel combination of PGKE and PTL for continual learning in vision-language models. It addresses relevant and practical challenges, and the experimental results show clear improvements on the chosen benchmark. However, the significance is limited by the reliance on a specific benchmark and a few cases in which it doesn't outperform other approaches (or very small improvement) which weakens some claims regarding performance. More robust evaluations and thorough documentation of implementation details could improve the paper's standing. While the paper has some weaknesses, I feel the paper contributes in a way that helps improve the architecture and parameter allocation within MoEs for CVL.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Bias-Aware Agent: Enhancing Fairness in AI-Driven Knowledge Retrieval
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21237v1)
- **Authors**: Karanbir Singh, William Ngu
- **Abstract**: Advancements in retrieving accessible information have evolved faster in the last few years compared to the decades since the internet's creation. Search engines, like Google, have been the number one way to find relevant data. They have always relied on the user's abilities to find the best information in its billions of links and sources at everybody's fingertips. The advent of large language models (LLMs) has completely transformed the field of information retrieval. The LLMs excel not only at retrieving relevant knowledge but also at summarizing it effectively, making information more accessible and consumable for users. On top of it, the rise of AI Agents has introduced another aspect to information retrieval i.e. dynamic information retrieval which enables the integration of real-time data such as weather forecasts, and financial data with the knowledge base to curate context-aware knowledge. However, despite these advancements the agents remain susceptible to issues of bias and fairness, challenges deeply rooted within the knowledge base and training of LLMs. This study introduces a novel approach to bias-aware knowledge retrieval by leveraging agentic framework and the innovative use of bias detectors as tools to identify and highlight inherent biases in the retrieved content. By empowering users with transparency and awareness, this approach aims to foster more equitable information systems and promote the development of responsible AI.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a Bias-Aware Agent framework designed to enhance fairness in AI-driven knowledge retrieval systems. It addresses the challenge of bias in information retrieved by Large Language Models (LLMs) and Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems. The framework leverages an agentic architecture using LangGraph and a ReAct-based agent, integrating a bias detection tool (specifically, a pre-trained text classification model called Dbias) to analyze retrieved content. The agent reasons about a user query, retrieves relevant information from a vector store, assesses the content for bias using the bias detection tool, and provides a summary answer along with a bias analysis to the user. The experimental results showcase the agent's ability to detect bias with reasonable accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main novelty lies in the proposed agentic framework that integrates bias detection as a modular tool within the information retrieval process. This allows for a dynamic and context-aware evaluation of bias. While existing work focuses on pre-processing, in-processing, or post-processing techniques for bias mitigation, this work offers a unique agentic approach to bias detection within RAG architectures.  The idea of using an agent to dynamically check for bias within retrieved content is a fairly new idea. However, the individual components like using a bias detection model are not novel. The use of LangGraph, while convenient, doesn't contribute significant novelty itself.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses an important problem: the persistence of bias in AI systems, especially information retrieval. Providing users with awareness of potential biases in retrieved information is a significant step towards promoting more equitable and responsible AI. By making bias detection transparent, it empowers users to make informed decisions and critically evaluate the information presented. This is especially relevant in sensitive domains where biased information could have significant societal impacts.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear and well-defined framework architecture.
    *   The modular design allows for easy integration of different bias detection models.
    *   Demonstrates the framework's effectiveness through experiments with real-world queries.
    *   Addresses a critical issue in responsible AI development.
    *   The system prioritizes utility by still providing the information with bias detected instead of censoring.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The evaluation focuses mainly on bias detection accuracy and doesn't deeply explore the impact of bias awareness on user behavior or decision-making.
    *   The paper relies on a single, specific bias detection model (Dbias).  The generalizability of the framework to other types of bias or other bias detection tools is not fully explored.
    *   The bias detection model itself has inherent biases, and the paper should discuss the impact of this on the overall framework performance.
    *   The chosen queries are limited in scope and complexity.
    *   Limited quantitative results for results of the agent; qualitative results make the argument of a Bias Aware Agent.
    *   Lack of comparisons to alternative bias detection and mitigation strategies.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could inspire further research into agentic approaches for bias detection and mitigation in AI systems. The framework provides a useful starting point for developing more sophisticated and context-aware bias analysis tools. Also, it could influence the adoption of similar techniques in practical applications like search engines, news aggregators, and question-answering systems. The agent's utility-first approach can be useful in other AI contexts to allow for transparency to the AI's flaws.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the paper's novelty and significance, I am assigning it a score of **7**. The concept of integrating bias detection into an agentic knowledge retrieval framework is novel and relevant. The paper tackles a real-world problem and presents a clear architectural approach. However, the evaluation is limited, and more work is needed to explore the framework's robustness, generalizability, and impact on user behavior. Also, the paper would benefit from comparisons to other bias detection strategies. While the work has potential influence in the field, it is not a transformative contribution at this stage.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### ResearchBench: Benchmarking LLMs in Scientific Discovery via Inspiration-Based Task Decomposition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21248v1)
- **Authors**: Yujie Liu, Zonglin Yang, Tong Xie, Jinjie Ni, Ben Gao, Yuqiang Li, Shixiang Tang, Wanli Ouyang, Erik Cambria, Dongzhan Zhou
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated potential in assisting scientific research, yet their ability to discover high-quality research hypotheses remains unexamined due to the lack of a dedicated benchmark. To address this gap, we introduce the first large-scale benchmark for evaluating LLMs with a near-sufficient set of sub-tasks of scientific discovery: inspiration retrieval, hypothesis composition, and hypothesis ranking. We develop an automated framework that extracts critical components - research questions, background surveys, inspirations, and hypotheses - from scientific papers across 12 disciplines, with expert validation confirming its accuracy. To prevent data contamination, we focus exclusively on papers published in 2024, ensuring minimal overlap with LLM pretraining data. Our evaluation reveals that LLMs perform well in retrieving inspirations, an out-of-distribution task, suggesting their ability to surface novel knowledge associations. This positions LLMs as "research hypothesis mines", capable of facilitating automated scientific discovery by generating innovative hypotheses at scale with minimal human intervention.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, "ResearchBench: Benchmarking LLMs in Scientific Discovery via Inspiration-Based Task Decomposition":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ResearchBench, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to perform key tasks in scientific discovery. Recognizing the lack of specific benchmarks for this purpose, the authors decompose the scientific discovery process into three sub-tasks: inspiration retrieval, hypothesis composition, and hypothesis ranking.  The benchmark leverages a novel automated agentic framework that extracts relevant information (research questions, background, inspirations, hypotheses) from scientific papers across 12 disciplines. To avoid data contamination, the benchmark is restricted to papers published in 2024, minimizing overlap with the training data of most current LLMs. The authors conduct extensive experiments, comparing several popular LLMs on the three sub-tasks. Their results reveal that LLMs are surprisingly effective at inspiration retrieval (an out-of-distribution task), suggesting an ability to identify novel knowledge associations. They also demonstrate good performance on hypothesis composition and ranking. Based on these findings, the authors propose LLMs can be viewed as "research hypothesis mines," capable of generating innovative hypotheses at scale with minimal human intervention.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in its proposal of a new benchmark tailored explicitly for evaluating LLMs in scientific discovery. While the task decomposition (inspiration retrieval, hypothesis composition, ranking) has been explored in previous, smaller-scale work, this is the first large-scale benchmark to operationalize this framework across multiple scientific disciplines. The automated agentic framework for data extraction and the focus on recent publications to avoid data contamination are also significant strengths.

*   **Significance:** Scientific discovery is a computationally challenging but high-impact problem.  If LLMs can genuinely assist in this process, it could have transformative effects on research across various fields. The paper's benchmark directly addresses a critical gap in evaluating LLM capabilities for this crucial application. The decomposition into sub-tasks allows for a more granular understanding of LLM performance and helps pinpoint areas for future research. The "research hypothesis mine" concept is intriguing and provides a novel perspective on LLM potential. The study's focus on out-of-distribution inspiration retrieval is particularly insightful, as it highlights the LLMs' ability to connect seemingly unrelated pieces of knowledge.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined problem and clear motivation:** The authors clearly articulate the problem of evaluating LLMs for scientific discovery and the need for a dedicated benchmark.
    *   **Rigorous methodology:** The automated agentic framework and the experimental design are well-structured and implemented. The efforts to avoid data contamination are commendable.
    *   **Large-scale evaluation:** The benchmark covers 12 diverse disciplines and involves a substantial number of papers, providing a robust basis for analysis.
    *   **Insightful analysis:** The authors carefully analyze LLM performance on each sub-task, identifying strengths, weaknesses, and potential bottlenecks.
    *   **Practical implications:**  The "research hypothesis mine" concept offers a valuable perspective on how LLMs can be used to accelerate scientific discovery.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Subjectivity in evaluation:** While the automated framework is valuable, some aspects of the evaluation (e.g., the quality of generated hypotheses) likely involve some level of subjectivity.  The expert evaluation helps mitigate this, but further measures to ensure consistency and inter-rater reliability could be beneficial.

    *   **Reliance on existing papers:** The benchmark's current design relies heavily on extracting information from existing scientific papers. This inherently limits the novelty of the "discovered" hypotheses to some extent.  Exploring the ability of LLMs to generate truly *de novo* hypotheses, not directly derived from existing literature, could be a valuable extension.
    *   **Limited analysis on hypothesis "validity":** Although the benchmark assesses the performance of the LLM in generation and hypothesis ranking, the actual validity of the final generated hypothesis (are the produced hypotheses 'correct'?) still needs to be evaluated by human experts in a real-world setting.
    *  **Position Bias problem:** The position bias problem found in hypothesis ranking is a problem that the paper tries to solve, but there is no evidence that the process alleviates the problem effectively.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's contribution is likely to have a significant impact on research related to LLMs and scientific discovery. The ResearchBench benchmark will likely become a valuable tool for evaluating and comparing different models and training strategies. The findings will also guide future research efforts aimed at improving the ability of LLMs to assist in scientific discovery.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** ResearchBench is a valuable and well-executed contribution to the field. It addresses a critical gap by providing a large-scale benchmark for evaluating LLMs in scientific discovery. The work is novel, rigorous, and provides significant insights into the capabilities and limitations of current LLMs. While some limitations exist, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses, making it a significant step towards enabling more automated and efficient scientific research.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### vGamba: Attentive State Space Bottleneck for efficient Long-range Dependencies in Visual Recognition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21262v1)
- **Authors**: Yunusa Haruna, Adamu Lawan
- **Abstract**: Capturing long-range dependencies efficiently is essential for visual recognition tasks, yet existing methods face limitations. Convolutional neural networks (CNNs) struggle with restricted receptive fields, while Vision Transformers (ViTs) achieve global context and long-range modeling at a high computational cost. State-space models (SSMs) offer an alternative, but their application in vision remains underexplored. This work introduces vGamba, a hybrid vision backbone that integrates SSMs with attention mechanisms to enhance efficiency and expressiveness. At its core, the Gamba bottleneck block that includes, Gamba Cell, an adaptation of Mamba for 2D spatial structures, alongside a Multi-Head Self-Attention (MHSA) mechanism and a Gated Fusion Module for effective feature representation. The interplay of these components ensures that vGamba leverages the low computational demands of SSMs while maintaining the accuracy of attention mechanisms for modeling long-range dependencies in vision tasks. Additionally, the Fusion module enables seamless interaction between these components. Extensive experiments on classification, detection, and segmentation tasks demonstrate that vGamba achieves a superior trade-off between accuracy and computational efficiency, outperforming several existing models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the vGamba paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces vGamba, a hybrid vision backbone that combines State-Space Models (SSMs), specifically an adaptation of Mamba, with attention mechanisms.  The key innovation is the "Gamba Block," a bottleneck structure composed of a Gamba Cell (a Mamba adaptation for 2D spatial data with positional embeddings), a Multi-Head Self-Attention (MHSA) mechanism, and a Gated Fusion Module. The goal is to leverage the efficiency of SSMs for long-range dependency modeling while retaining the accuracy and global context understanding of attention mechanisms.  The Gated Fusion Module plays a critical role in seamlessly integrating the outputs of the SSM and attention components. Experiments across classification, detection, and segmentation tasks demonstrate that vGamba achieves a better trade-off between accuracy and computational efficiency compared to existing CNNs, Transformers, and pure SSM approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Incremental but meaningful:** The paper builds on existing work in SSMs (Mamba) and Transformers, adapting Mamba for vision tasks and combining it with attention. The novelty isn't a radical new theory or model but rather a clever architectural combination and adaptation. The specific implementation details of the Gamba Cell (incorporating 2D positional embeddings and spatial context awareness) and the Gated Fusion Module appear to be original contributions.
*   **Hybrid Approach:** The idea of combining SSMs and attention mechanisms isn't entirely new in NLP, but its application to vision, particularly with the proposed Gamba Block structure, is a valuable contribution.

**Significance:**

*   **Performance Trade-off:**  The paper demonstrates a convincing trade-off between accuracy and efficiency. The experiments show that vGamba can achieve comparable or even superior performance to existing models with lower computational cost and fewer parameters. This is significant because it addresses a major bottleneck in deploying deep learning models for visual recognition, especially in resource-constrained environments.
*   **Wide Applicability:** The experimental results across image classification, object detection, and semantic segmentation suggest that vGamba is a versatile backbone suitable for a variety of vision tasks.
*   **Potential for Future Research:** The paper opens up avenues for further research into hybrid SSM-attention architectures for vision. It provides a strong baseline for future investigations into more efficient and effective ways to model long-range dependencies in images.
*   **Limitations:** The performance gains compared to current SOA especially in object detection and segmentation are not substantially large, therefore the impact is not disruptive. The ablation studies could be more extensive, examining the individual contributions of different parts of the Gamba Cell in more detail. The sensitivity to hyperparameters in the fusion module, although mentioned, is a limitation that needs more investigation.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper represents a solid engineering contribution with a clear and well-motivated architecture. The experimental results are thorough and support the claims of improved efficiency and accuracy. While the core ideas aren't entirely groundbreaking, the specific implementation and demonstrated performance trade-off make it a valuable addition to the field. Given the incremental nature of the novelty but the practical significance of the results, and with consideration of some limitations that require further study, a score of 7 is warranted.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Delving Deep into Semantic Relation Distillation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21269v1)
- **Authors**: Zhaoyi Yan, Kangjun Liu, Qixiang Ye
- **Abstract**: Knowledge distillation has become a cornerstone technique in deep learning, facilitating the transfer of knowledge from complex models to lightweight counterparts. Traditional distillation approaches focus on transferring knowledge at the instance level, but fail to capture nuanced semantic relationships within the data. In response, this paper introduces a novel methodology, Semantics-based Relation Knowledge Distillation (SeRKD), which reimagines knowledge distillation through a semantics-relation lens among each sample. By leveraging semantic components, \ie, superpixels, SeRKD enables a more comprehensive and context-aware transfer of knowledge, which skillfully integrates superpixel-based semantic extraction with relation-based knowledge distillation for a sophisticated model compression and distillation. Particularly, the proposed method is naturally relevant in the domain of Vision Transformers (ViTs), where visual tokens serve as fundamental units of representation. Experimental evaluations on benchmark datasets demonstrate the superiority of SeRKD over existing methods, underscoring its efficacy in enhancing model performance and generalization capabilities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel knowledge distillation method called Semantics-based Relation Knowledge Distillation (SeRKD).  SeRKD focuses on transferring knowledge at a more granular semantic level by leveraging superpixels to extract semantic components within images. These semantic components, acting as "semantic-superpixel tokens," are then used to perform relation-based knowledge distillation. The method is particularly tailored for Vision Transformers (ViTs), where visual tokens naturally align with this approach. Experiments on ImageNet and other datasets demonstrate that SeRKD outperforms existing knowledge distillation methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of using superpixels to extract semantic information and subsequently perform relation-based knowledge distillation is reasonably novel. Most existing relation-based KD methods operate at the instance level or on raw token representations. Shifting the focus to semantically meaningful regions (superpixels) provides a potentially more effective mechanism for capturing and transferring knowledge. However, it builds upon existing superpixel methods like SSN, SPIN, and STViT, and combines them with a standard RKD framework. The innovation is primarily in the combination and application of these techniques to the problem of KD, especially for ViTs.

* **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing a specific limitation of instance-level and raw token-based knowledge distillation – the failure to capture nuanced semantic relationships. SeRKD's improved performance, as evidenced by the experimental results, suggests that incorporating semantic context into the distillation process can lead to better student models. This is particularly relevant for ViTs, where the token-based architecture can benefit from a more structured semantic representation. The transfer learning results further solidify the generalization capabilities of the method. The ablation studies provide a good understanding of the contributions from different components of SeRKD.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing KD techniques and motivates the need for a semantics-aware approach.
    * **Well-Defined Method:** The SeRKD method is well-defined and explained, with clear descriptions of the superpixel extraction process and the adaptation of relation-based distillation.
    * **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results on ImageNet and downstream datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of SeRKD compared to existing methods. Ablation studies help understand the individual contributions of different components.
    * **Relevance to ViTs:** The method is specifically tailored for ViTs, which are becoming increasingly prevalent in computer vision.
    * **Comprehensive Ablation Studies:** Ablation studies help understand the contribution of each module.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Improvement:**  While the performance gains are statistically significant in many cases, the absolute improvements may be considered incremental compared to some state-of-the-art knowledge distillation methods. Some improvements are marginal over existing methods.
    * **Dependence on Superpixel Quality:** The performance of SeRKD is likely dependent on the quality of the superpixel segmentation.  Poor superpixel segmentation could hinder the extraction of meaningful semantic information. The impact is only discussed but not measured directly.
    * **Limited Visualizations:** While there is a visualization of learned superpixels, more visualization of relations between superpixels would further strengthen the paper.
    * **Computational Complexity:** The complexity analysis reveals that RKdAngle has higher complexity, limiting the application to larger L.

* **Potential Influence:** SeRKD has the potential to influence the field by highlighting the importance of semantic context in knowledge distillation. The approach could inspire further research into methods that incorporate semantic information into the distillation process, especially for ViTs and other transformer-based models. Furthermore, the idea of using superpixels as a semantic building block could be explored in other areas of deep learning beyond knowledge distillation.

**Justification for Score:**

While SeRKD introduces a novel way to perform relation-based knowledge distillation by incorporating semantic cues through superpixels, its novelty and impact can be seen as incremental. It builds on established methods (RKD, Superpixel techniques), and the improvements, while noticeable and often statistically significant, aren't groundbreaking. The computational complexity is potentially limiting, and the dependence on superpixel quality is a possible bottleneck.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Reinforced Model Merging
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21272v1)
- **Authors**: Jiaqi Han, Jingwen Ye, Shunyu Liu, Haofei Zhang, Jie Song, Zunlei Feng, Mingli Song
- **Abstract**: The success of large language models has garnered widespread attention for model merging techniques, especially training-free methods which combine model capabilities within the parameter space. However, two challenges remain: (1) uniform treatment of all parameters leads to performance degradation; (2) search-based algorithms are often inefficient. In this paper, we present an innovative framework termed Reinforced Model Merging (RMM), which encompasses an environment and agent tailored for merging tasks. These components interact to execute layer-wise merging actions, aiming to search the optimal merging architecture. Notably, RMM operates without any gradient computations on the original models, rendering it feasible for edge devices. Furthermore, by utilizing data subsets during the evaluation process, we addressed the bottleneck in the reward feedback phase, thereby accelerating RMM by up to 100 times. Extensive experiments demonstrate that RMM achieves state-of-the-art performance across various vision and NLP datasets and effectively overcomes the limitations of the existing baseline methods. Our code is available at https://github.com/WuDiHJQ/Reinforced-Model-Merging.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Reinforced Model Merging":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Reinforced Model Merging (RMM), a novel framework for merging pre-trained models without requiring gradient computations on the original models.  RMM uses reinforcement learning (RL) where an agent navigates the layers of the models, selects merging actions (choosing which model's layer or a merging operator), and is rewarded based on the performance of the resulting merged model. The method is designed to address two key challenges in model merging: the uniform treatment of all parameters, which can degrade performance, and the inefficiency of search-based algorithms.  The paper also introduces Dynamic Average Reward (DAR) mechanism to speed up the RL training by using a small subset of the training data for the reward computation. Experimental results demonstrate that RMM achieves state-of-the-art performance across vision and NLP datasets while being computationally efficient.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core idea of using RL for model merging is indeed novel.  Prior works predominantly rely on heuristic merging strategies or search-based approaches that involve evaluating merged models on the full dataset, which is computationally expensive.  RMM provides a more principled and automated approach to finding the optimal merging architecture. The DAR mechanism addresses the efficiency bottleneck, a critical practical concern. The combination of RL and DAR tailored for the merging setting represents a significant step forward.

*   **Significance:**  The paper's significance lies in its potential to make model merging more practical and accessible.  The ability to merge models efficiently, even on resource-constrained devices (as suggested by the gradient-free operation and the DAR-based reward evaluation), opens up possibilities for deploying more powerful models in edge computing scenarios.  The improved performance compared to existing methods also suggests that RMM can identify better merging architectures, leading to more effective knowledge transfer and combined capabilities.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The RL framework provides a structured way to optimize model merging.
    *   DAR mechanism significantly improves the efficiency of the merging process.
    *   The paper demonstrates strong experimental results across various vision and NLP tasks.
    *   The method does not require gradient computation on original models, which makes it feasible to deploy on edge devices.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper could provide more detailed explanations of the design choices of the RL agent and environment (e.g., the specific reward function, the action space). The intuition behind specific RL reward design deserves discussion.
    *   While the DAR mechanism reduces computational cost, the effectiveness of the chosen subset for evaluation is essential. A discussion or ablation on the characteristics of the subset that are crucial for effective DAR is missing.
    *   The number of parameters for RL is minimal, and the RL training is quick, but there is no mention about RL hyperparameter selection. This adds questions about the robustness of the proposed framework.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of model merging by introducing a new paradigm based on RL.  It also raises interesting questions about the design of effective merging strategies and the trade-offs between performance and efficiency.  The framework's extensibility could inspire further research into integrating other advanced merging techniques and applying it to more complex scenarios. The focus on efficiency and edge deployment is timely and relevant.

*   **Justification for Score:** While the paper presents a significant contribution, some details need to be explored. The proposed methodology is novel and has the potential to solve several issues. The speed up obtained by using a reduced set of training data is also crucial. However, there are some questions regarding experimental results and robustness of the proposed framework. Therefore, the paper deserves a score of 7, reflecting its significant novelty and potential, but also acknowledging the need for more detailed investigation.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Zero-Shot Visual Concept Blending Without Text Guidance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21277v1)
- **Authors**: Hiroya Makino, Takahiro Yamaguchi, Hiroyuki Sakai
- **Abstract**: We propose a novel, zero-shot image generation technique called "Visual Concept Blending" that provides fine-grained control over which features from multiple reference images are transferred to a source image. If only a single reference image is available, it is difficult to isolate which specific elements should be transferred. However, using multiple reference images, the proposed approach distinguishes between common and unique features by selectively incorporating them into a generated output. By operating within a partially disentangled Contrastive Language-Image Pre-training (CLIP) embedding space (from IP-Adapter), our method enables the flexible transfer of texture, shape, motion, style, and more abstract conceptual transformations without requiring additional training or text prompts. We demonstrate its effectiveness across a diverse range of tasks, including style transfer, form metamorphosis, and conceptual transformations, showing how subtle or abstract attributes (e.g., brushstroke style, aerodynamic lines, and dynamism) can be seamlessly combined into a new image. In a user study, participants accurately recognized which features were intended to be transferred. Its simplicity, flexibility, and high-level control make Visual Concept Blending valuable for creative fields such as art, design, and content creation, where combining specific visual qualities from multiple inspirations is crucial.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Visual Concept Blending," a novel zero-shot image generation technique. Unlike conventional visual concept translation methods that rely on a single reference image and often struggle to isolate specific transferable features, this approach leverages multiple reference images. By operating within the CLIP embedding space (using IP-Adapter), the method distinguishes between common and unique features present in the reference images. This allows for flexible control over transferring attributes like texture, shape, style, and abstract concepts to a source image without additional training or text prompts. Experiments demonstrate the method's effectiveness across various tasks, including style transfer, form metamorphosis, and conceptual transformations, and a user study validates that the intended features are accurately transferred.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty:** The core idea of using multiple reference images to disentangle and selectively transfer visual features is a significant improvement over single-reference methods.  This directly addresses a key limitation in prior visual concept translation research. The operation in the CLIP embedding space with IP-Adapter is clever and facilitates more abstract and flexible transfers compared to pixel-level or feature-map level approaches.
    *   **Zero-shot capability:** Avoiding additional training or text prompts is a major advantage. It makes the method flexible and readily applicable to diverse scenarios without requiring extensive dataset curation or fine-tuning.
    *   **Simplicity and Robustness:** The method is presented as operating solely within the pre-trained CLIP embedding space, leading to a simpler and more robust framework. This contrasts with approaches involving complex training procedures or architectural modifications to diffusion models.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents both qualitative results (demonstrating diverse transformations) and a user study (quantifying the accuracy of feature transfer).  The parameter sensitivity analysis is also a welcome addition.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Disentanglement:** The method's effectiveness heavily depends on the assumption that the CLIP embedding space within IP-Adapter is partially disentangled. While the paper cites studies supporting this, it doesn't fully address potential limitations or biases inherent in the CLIP representation. A more rigorous analysis of the degree of disentanglement achieved and its impact on performance would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Decoder Limitations:** The paper acknowledges that the IP-Adapter decoder's representational capacity can limit the quality of generated images, especially for complex or unusual combinations of features. While this is a common issue in generative models, a more thorough discussion of the types of artifacts or limitations introduced by the decoder would be beneficial.
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity:** While the paper discusses parameter sensitivity, the need for parameter tuning remains a practical limitation. Automated parameter selection methods would significantly improve the usability of the approach.
    *   **Limited Comparison:** While the paper includes comparisons to existing methods, these are primarily qualitative. A quantitative comparison against state-of-the-art style transfer or image manipulation techniques would provide a more objective assessment of the proposed method's performance.
    *   **User Study Concerns:** The user study is potentially simplistic and prone to biases.  The correct selection may only be based on a single discernible difference in the output image, as opposed to a more complex understanding of the concept blending.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper offers a valuable contribution to the field of image generation and manipulation. The core idea of using multiple references for visual concept blending is innovative and addresses a key challenge in prior work. The zero-shot capability and relative simplicity of the method make it attractive for practical applications.
    *   The paper's findings could inspire further research into disentangled representations and their use in controlling generative models. It also opens up new possibilities for creative applications in art, design, and content creation.

**Justification:**

The paper showcases a novel approach to visual concept blending with promising results. The idea of utilizing multiple reference images to disentangle features and enable more controlled transformations is the paper's most significant strength. However, its reliance on the disentanglement quality of CLIP embeddings and the limitations of the decoder, as well as the need for further quantitative evaluations, and the need for improvements in the user study, prevent it from achieving a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### R-PRM: Reasoning-Driven Process Reward Modeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21295v1)
- **Authors**: Shuaijie She, Junxiao Liu, Yifeng Liu, Jiajun Chen, Xin Huang, Shujian Huang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) inevitably make mistakes when performing step-by-step mathematical reasoning. Process Reward Models (PRMs) have emerged as a promising solution by evaluating each reasoning step. However, existing PRMs typically output evaluation scores directly, limiting both learning efficiency and evaluation accuracy, which is further exacerbated by the scarcity of annotated data. To address these issues, we propose Reasoning-Driven Process Reward Modeling (R-PRM). First, we leverage stronger LLMs to generate seed data from limited annotations, effectively bootstrapping our model's reasoning capabilities and enabling comprehensive step-by-step evaluation. Second, we further enhance performance through preference optimization, without requiring additional annotated data. Third, we introduce inference-time scaling to fully harness the model's reasoning potential. Extensive experiments demonstrate R-PRM's effectiveness: on ProcessBench and PRMBench, it surpasses strong baselines by 11.9 and 8.5 points in F1 scores, respectively. When applied to guide mathematical reasoning, R-PRM achieves consistent accuracy improvements of over 8.5 points across six challenging datasets. Further analysis reveals that R-PRM exhibits more comprehensive evaluation and stronger generalization capabilities, thereby highlighting its significant potential.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and rigorous evaluation of the paper "R-PRM: Reasoning-Driven Process Reward Modeling":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Reasoning-Driven Process Reward Modeling (R-PRM), a novel framework to enhance process reward models (PRMs) for evaluating step-by-step mathematical reasoning in large language models (LLMs). R-PRM addresses limitations of existing PRMs, such as data scarcity, direct evaluation limiting learning efficiency, and lack of interpretability. The framework comprises three key elements: (1) generating seed data by prompting stronger LLMs with limited human annotations, (2) generative evaluation paradigm for preference optimization, encouraging evaluation processes that lead to correct judgments, and (3) inference-time scaling, sampling multiple evaluation processes for robust assessment.  Experiments on ProcessBench and PRMBench show that R-PRM outperforms strong baselines, and when used to guide policy models, it achieves consistent accuracy improvements across challenging math datasets. The paper also highlights comprehensive evaluation coverage, enhanced generalization, and progressive accuracy improvements as additional advantages.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a significant advancement over existing PRMs. Addressing data scarcity through LLM-based seed data generation and preference optimization is a valuable contribution. The idea of a reasoning-driven approach, where the model not only provides a reward but also explains *why* a step is correct or incorrect, is a substantial improvement in interpretability and a departure from direct scoring methods. Inference-time scaling to enhance robustness is a logical extension that adds practical value.

*   **Significance:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of mathematical reasoning with LLMs. Improving the quality and interpretability of process-level evaluation is crucial for identifying and mitigating reasoning errors. R-PRM’s ability to guide policy models effectively could lead to more reliable and accurate problem-solving. The gains observed on benchmark datasets are substantial and practically important.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing PRMs.
    *   **Well-Defined Solution:** R-PRM's components are logically structured and easy to understand.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** Experiments are comprehensive and demonstrate the effectiveness of the framework across multiple datasets and evaluation settings. The ablation studies further highlight the importance of each component.
    *   **Interpretability:** One of the main strengths is the interpretability aspect of providing rationale for each judgement, which is a step towards building more trustful and reliable systems.
    *   **Scalability:** The inference-time scaling allows to trade off performance for computational resources.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Strong LLMs:** The seed data generation relies on the capabilities of a strong LLM (LLaMA3.3-70B in this case). This raises questions about the portability of the approach to settings where such LLMs are unavailable or computationally prohibitive.
    *   **Potential Bias:** The seed data generation from LLMs, even after consistency filtering, can introduce biases that might influence the learning process of R-PRM. This needs to be explored with a more detailed analysis.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Advanced Search:** While the paper explores Best-of-N and Guided Search, it acknowledges the potential of more sophisticated search algorithms like MCTS and Beam Search, leaving room for future research.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Even though inference-time scaling is beneficial, the need to run many evaluations can add up. This aspect could be addressed by future works for improved efficiency.

*   **Potential Influence:** R-PRM's reasoning-driven approach could influence the development of more interpretable and reliable PRMs. The techniques for seed data generation and preference optimization could be adopted in other areas where data scarcity is a problem. The inference-time scaling strategy could become a standard practice for enhancing the robustness of evaluation systems.

**Justification:** The paper makes a significant contribution by addressing the limitations of existing PRMs through innovative techniques, such as reasoning-driven evaluation and preference optimization.  The empirical results are compelling and demonstrate the practical benefits of R-PRM.  While the reliance on strong LLMs and potential bias are valid concerns, the overall novelty and significance of the work outweigh these limitations.

**Score: 8.5**

**Rigorous Rationale:** The paper earns a high score due to its innovative approach, clear problem definition, comprehensive experiments, and potential influence on the field. While some weaknesses exist, they do not diminish the overall impact of the work. The move from "black box" reward models to reasoning-driven evaluations significantly enhances the trustworthiness and effectiveness of process-level reward modeling, marking a substantial advancement in the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### InternVL-X: Advancing and Accelerating InternVL Series with Efficient Visual Token Compression
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21307v1)
- **Authors**: Dongchen Lu, Yuyao Sun, Zilu Zhang, Leping Huang, Jianliang Zeng, Mao Shu, Huo Cao
- **Abstract**: Most multimodal large language models (MLLMs) treat visual tokens as "a sequence of text", integrating them with text tokens into a large language model (LLM). However, a great quantity of visual tokens significantly increases the demand for computational resources and time. In this paper, we propose InternVL-X, which outperforms the InternVL model in both performance and efficiency by incorporating three visual token compression methods. First, we propose a novel vision-language projector, PVTC. This component integrates adjacent visual embeddings to form a local query and utilizes the transformed CLS token as a global query, then performs point-to-region cross-attention through these local and global queries to more effectively convert visual features. Second, we present a layer-wise visual token compression module, LVTC, which compresses tokens in the LLM shallow layers and then expands them through upsampling and residual connections in the deeper layers. This significantly enhances the model computational efficiency. Futhermore, we propose an efficient high resolution slicing method, RVTC, which dynamically adjusts the number of visual tokens based on image area or length filtering. RVTC greatly enhances training efficiency with only a slight reduction in performance. By utilizing 20% or fewer visual tokens, InternVL-X achieves state-of-the-art performance on 7 public MLLM benchmarks, and improves the average metric by 2.34% across 12 tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the InternVL-X paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces InternVL-X, an advancement of the InternVL series of multimodal large language models (MLLMs).  InternVL-X focuses on improving both the performance and efficiency of processing visual tokens by incorporating three visual token compression methods:

1.  **PVTC (Projector Visual Token Compressor):** Integrates adjacent visual embeddings to form a local query and a global query (from the CLS token), using point-to-region cross-attention for effective visual feature conversion.

2.  **LVTC (Layer-wise Visual Token Compressor):** Compresses tokens in the LLM's shallow layers and expands them in deeper layers with upsampling and residual connections to enhance computational efficiency.

3.  **RVTC (Resolution Visual Token Compressor):** Dynamically adjusts the number of visual tokens based on image area or length filtering to improve training efficiency.

The combined effect allows InternVL-X to achieve state-of-the-art results on several MLLM benchmarks, utilizing a smaller number of visual tokens.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents incremental novelty by combining three distinct token compression techniques tailored to the MLLM pipeline.  PVTC offers a refined cross-attention mechanism, LVTC uses layer-wise compression informed by observed attention patterns, and RVTC provides an adaptive resolution strategy. While each component builds upon existing research, their integration within the InternVL framework presents a potentially novel optimization strategy. However, the descriptions of each component sometimes lack sufficient detail to fully judge their individual innovative aspects (e.g., the specific architecture of "point-to-region cross-attention"). Also, its novel combination of well-known concepts.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing the computational bottlenecks in MLLMs due to the large number of visual tokens. Improving efficiency without sacrificing performance is a crucial step towards more practical and scalable MLLMs. InternVL-X demonstrates that a coordinated approach to token compression, targeting different stages of processing (projection, LLM layers, and image slicing), can yield significant improvements. The reported state-of-the-art results on several benchmarks further reinforce the practical importance. Also, the paper mentions source code.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper tackles an important and relevant problem in the MLLM field.
    *   The combination of three different compression techniques is a solid approach.
    *   The experimental results are comprehensive, including comparisons to other models and ablation studies.
    *   Claims are adequately supported by results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the concept of combining compression strategies is novel, details of PVTC, LVTC and RVTC are a little generic and lack specific depth to distinguish them substantially from prior work.
    *   Some descriptions are vague.
    *   The paper lacks in-depth analysis of failure cases or limitations of the approach. The POPE benchmark result, where performance degrades slightly, could have been further explored.
    *   The ablation studies, while present, could be more detailed. For example, varying key parameters within each module.

*   **Potential Influence:** The InternVL-X approach could influence future research by:
    *   Encouraging a more holistic view of visual token compression in MLLMs.
    *   Providing a practical framework for implementing and evaluating different compression strategies.
    *   Setting a new benchmark for performance and efficiency in MLLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the incremental but practically important improvements in performance and efficiency, the novelty of the combined approach, the strong experimental validation, and the potential impact on the field, a score of 7 is justified. While the individual components might not be groundbreaking on their own, their synergistic integration and the resulting state-of-the-art performance warrant a positive assessment. A lower score would be appropriate if the individual components demonstrated a clear lack of originality or if the experimental results were not sufficiently compelling. A higher score would require more substantial and fundamentally novel algorithmic contributions within each compression module.

### HORT: Monocular Hand-held Objects Reconstruction with Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21313v1)
- **Authors**: Zerui Chen, Rolandos Alexandros Potamias, Shizhe Chen, Cordelia Schmid
- **Abstract**: Reconstructing hand-held objects in 3D from monocular images remains a significant challenge in computer vision. Most existing approaches rely on implicit 3D representations, which produce overly smooth reconstructions and are time-consuming to generate explicit 3D shapes. While more recent methods directly reconstruct point clouds with diffusion models, the multi-step denoising makes high-resolution reconstruction inefficient. To address these limitations, we propose a transformer-based model to efficiently reconstruct dense 3D point clouds of hand-held objects. Our method follows a coarse-to-fine strategy, first generating a sparse point cloud from the image and progressively refining it into a dense representation using pixel-aligned image features. To enhance reconstruction accuracy, we integrate image features with 3D hand geometry to jointly predict the object point cloud and its pose relative to the hand. Our model is trained end-to-end for optimal performance. Experimental results on both synthetic and real datasets demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art accuracy with much faster inference speed, while generalizing well to in-the-wild images.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "HORT: Monocular Hand-held Objects Reconstruction with Transformers":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HORT, a novel transformer-based framework for reconstructing 3D point clouds of hand-held objects from single RGB images. The method adopts a coarse-to-fine strategy: first, a sparse point cloud is generated from the image and hand features; then, it's progressively refined into a dense representation using pixel-aligned image features.  The model leverages both image and 3D hand geometry to jointly predict the object point cloud and its pose relative to the hand. The HORT model is trained end-to-end.  Experimental results on synthetic and real datasets demonstrate superior reconstruction accuracy and significantly faster inference speed compared to state-of-the-art methods, while generalizing well to in-the-wild images.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Strengths:**  The core novelty lies in the efficient coarse-to-fine architecture.  The transformer-based decoder, with its pixel-aligned feature extraction for dense point cloud refinement, appears to be a significant contribution to address the limitations of implicit function-based methods (over-smoothing, slow inference) and diffusion model approaches (high computational cost due to iterative denoising).  The integration of both image and detailed hand geometry information (transformed into multiple local coordinate systems) within the transformer architecture is another notable aspect.
    *   **Weaknesses:**  The individual components (e.g., image encoder using DINOv2, PointNet for hand feature extraction, transformer architecture) are not entirely novel on their own. The DINOv2 is a pre-trained model, and the PointNet is also another pre-existing approach. The innovation comes from the specific *combination and configuration* of these components within the overall framework. It builds upon existing methods (e.g. D-SCO) and improves their performance but isn't conceptually revolutionary.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Strengths:** The practical benefits of HORT are significant. Faster inference times without sacrificing accuracy are crucial for real-time applications like robotics and human-computer interaction.  The demonstrated generalization ability to "in-the-wild" images further enhances the potential impact. The results on the four diverse benchmarks and the ablation studies thoroughly support the claims. The comparative analyses with state-of-the-art methods are compelling. The model is robust in recovering in cases of noisy hand poses.
    *   **Weaknesses:** While faster than previous methods, the quantitative results on complex real-world datasets like MOW are still not perfect, indicating that there is room for improvement. Some failure cases are also showcased.

*   **Clarity and Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly presents the methodology, experimental setup, and results. The figures are helpful in understanding the architecture and performance. The ablation studies and comparisons are well-structured and easy to follow.

*   **Reproducibility:** The provision of code and a project website enhances the reproducibility of the results.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to influence the direction of research in hand-object interaction and 3D reconstruction.  The efficient, transformer-based architecture may inspire new approaches for balancing accuracy and speed in other computer vision tasks. It could also be used as strong initialization for more robust optimizaiton methods.

**Justification for Score:**

While HORT presents a significant improvement over existing methods in terms of efficiency and accuracy for monocular hand-held object reconstruction, it does not introduce groundbreaking or entirely new components. The architecture is novel in its combination of existing components (pre-trained DINOv2, PointNet for hand features, Transformer architecture), and the performance boost and real-time potential are highly valuable.

Score: 7

**Rigorous Rationale for the score of 7:**
A score of 7 reflects the substantial engineering contribution of the paper, clearly demonstrating a significant practical improvement over existing approaches by carefully assembling and training a system of known sub-components. The gains in terms of runtime while retaining the accuracy level make the paper above average (6 or above). While the combination of components might have been straight-forward for an expert in the field, the resulting improvement is quite significant and novel, so we increased the score to 7.

*A higher score (8 or above) would be warranted if:*
*   A completely novel architectural element or learning technique were introduced.
*   The approach tackled a significantly harder or unaddressed aspect of the problem (e.g., robust handling of extreme occlusions, object textures or totally unknown objects).

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Tricking Retrievers with Influential Tokens: An Efficient Black-Box Corpus Poisoning Attack
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21315v1)
- **Authors**: Cheng Wang, Yiwei Wang, Yujun Cai, Bryan Hooi
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems enhance large language models by incorporating external knowledge, addressing issues like outdated internal knowledge and hallucination. However, their reliance on external knowledge bases makes them vulnerable to corpus poisoning attacks, where adversarial passages can be injected to manipulate retrieval results. Existing methods for crafting such passages, such as random token replacement or training inversion models, are often slow and computationally expensive, requiring either access to retriever's gradients or large computational resources. To address these limitations, we propose Dynamic Importance-Guided Genetic Algorithm (DIGA), an efficient black-box method that leverages two key properties of retrievers: insensitivity to token order and bias towards influential tokens. By focusing on these characteristics, DIGA dynamically adjusts its genetic operations to generate effective adversarial passages with significantly reduced time and memory usage. Our experimental evaluation shows that DIGA achieves superior efficiency and scalability compared to existing methods, while maintaining comparable or better attack success rates across multiple datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Dynamic Importance-Guided Genetic Algorithm (DIGA), a novel black-box corpus poisoning attack against Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems. DIGA exploits the insensitivity of dense retrievers to token order and their bias towards influential tokens. It dynamically adjusts genetic operations in its algorithm based on token importance (calculated using TF-IDF) to generate effective adversarial passages. The method is shown to be more computationally efficient and scalable than existing approaches (HotFlip and Vec2Text) while maintaining comparable or better attack success rates across multiple datasets. The paper emphasizes that DIGA doesn't require white-box access to the retriever's gradients or extensive training, making it more practical for real-world scenarios. The paper also explores the transferability of the generated adversarial passages across different datasets and retrievers, and investigates the impact of initialization methods, passage lengths, and indirect prompt injection attacks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its efficient black-box approach to corpus poisoning. Existing methods either require white-box access (HotFlip), are computationally expensive (both HotFlip and Vec2Text), or require training of separate models for specific datasets (Vec2Text). DIGA uses a genetic algorithm guided by token importance to achieve a good balance between effectiveness and efficiency. The dynamic adjustment of genetic operations based on token importance is a unique aspect of the approach.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial vulnerability of RAG systems: their susceptibility to corpus poisoning.  The increased adoption of RAG systems makes them a more attractive target for malicious actors.  Demonstrating an efficient black-box attack like DIGA highlights the urgent need for developing robust defenses. The exploration of transferability is also valuable, as it shows how attacks generated on one dataset or against one retriever might be effective on others. The investigation into indirect prompt injection demonstrates the versatile nature of these attacks.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Efficiency:**  DIGA demonstrates a significant improvement in computational efficiency compared to baselines, making large-scale attacks more feasible.
    * **Black-box nature:**  The black-box nature of the attack increases its practical applicability.
    * **Scalability:**  The results show DIGA scales effectively with increasing numbers of adversarial passages.
    * **Clear experimental setup:**  The experimental setup is well-defined, with a thorough evaluation across multiple datasets and retrievers.
    * **Ablation studies:** Ablation studies explore the impact of different design choices (initialization, length).
    * **Addresses a relevant and timely problem:** Securing RAG systems is an important problem that is likely to become even more critical in the future.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited performance against white-box methods:** While DIGA outperforms black-box baselines, it generally doesn't match the performance of the white-box HotFlip attack when HotFlip is feasible.
    * **Limited transferability:** While it's investigated, the paper acknowledges a challenge with transferability across datasets for all methods, including DIGA. This means an attack optimized for one corpus may not be as effective on another.
    * **Perplexity analysis:** The perplexity analysis, while informative, doesn't fully capture the potential for more sophisticated adversarial passages that could be both influential and semantically fluent. It doesn't offer much new insight, and it just affirms that DIGA and HotFlip generate less "natural" text than Vec2Text.
    * **Reliance on TF-IDF:**  While TF-IDF is computationally efficient, it may not be the most accurate way to estimate token importance. More advanced methods, though computationally expensive, could potentially lead to even more effective attacks.

* **Potential Influence:** This paper is likely to influence future research in the area of adversarial attacks on RAG systems. It presents a practical and efficient attack method, which can be used as a benchmark for evaluating the effectiveness of defenses. It also highlights the importance of considering computational cost when designing attack strategies. The findings on transferability can guide the development of more generalizable attacks.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by presenting an efficient and scalable black-box attack against RAG systems. The exploitation of retriever properties through a dynamically adjusted genetic algorithm is a novel approach. However, it's not a perfect paper.  The limited transferability and the existing performance gap when compared to white-box approaches hold the paper back from being truly exceptional.  Additionally, although it's a neat trick to use and claim performance improvements as a result of using TF-IDF values, it is known to not be the *best* representation and a more complex approach may provide better results.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Large Language Models for Traffic and Transportation Research: Methodologies, State of the Art, and Future Opportunities
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21330v1)
- **Authors**: Yimo Yan, Yejia Liao, Guanhao Xu, Ruili Yao, Huiying Fan, Jingran Sun, Xia Wang, Jonathan Sprinkle, Ziyan An, Meiyi Ma, Xi Cheng, Tong Liu, Zemian Ke, Bo Zou, Matthew Barth, Yong-Hong Kuo
- **Abstract**: The rapid rise of Large Language Models (LLMs) is transforming traffic and transportation research, with significant advancements emerging between the years 2023 and 2025 -- a period marked by the inception and swift growth of adopting and adapting LLMs for various traffic and transportation applications. However, despite these significant advancements, a systematic review and synthesis of the existing studies remain lacking. To address this gap, this paper provides a comprehensive review of the methodologies and applications of LLMs in traffic and transportation, highlighting their ability to process unstructured textual data to advance transportation research. We explore key applications, including autonomous driving, travel behavior prediction, and general transportation-related queries, alongside methodologies such as zero- or few-shot learning, prompt engineering, and fine-tuning. Our analysis identifies critical research gaps. From the methodological perspective, many research gaps can be addressed by integrating LLMs with existing tools and refining LLM architectures. From the application perspective, we identify numerous opportunities for LLMs to tackle a variety of traffic and transportation challenges, building upon existing research. By synthesizing these findings, this review not only clarifies the current state of LLM adoption and adaptation in traffic and transportation but also proposes future research directions, paving the way for smarter and more sustainable transportation systems.
